## Vision (ratified 2026-09-24)

> **A permanently resident speech-native interlocutor hears, interprets and speaks. It has one external tool, the
> orchestrator, which supplies the cognition. Preserve speech-native state locally; export only the cognitive problem.**

```
                 ┌──────────────────────── SPEECH PLANE ─────────────────────────┐
 audio in ──────▶│ VOICE CONTROLLER (model-agnostic; lives in epyc-orchestrator) │
                 │  VAD · turn detection · barge-in · session · routing policy   │
                 │  context budget · degraded mode · event log                   │
                 │        │ Interlocutor backend interface (pluggable)           │
                 │        ├─ candidate: Step-Audio 2 mini   (bake-off lead)      │
                 │        ├─ candidate: Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B  (bake-off challenger)│
                 │        └─ cascade: whisper :9000 → (text) → qwentts :9002     │
 audio out ◀─────│           (baseline + permanent fallback)                     │
                 └───────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                 │ ONE tool: the voice-turn contract (streaming)
                 ┌───────────────▼──────── COGNITION PLANE ───────────────────────┐
                 │ ORCHESTRATOR: reasoning · retrieval · web · code · memory ·    │
                 │ tools · model routing. Replaceable without touching speech.    │
                 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The interlocutor and the orchestrator split the work like this.
- **The interlocutor** hears, reads the paralinguistic signal, handles timing and turn-taking, gives social and backchannel responses, and decides the delivery.
- **The orchestrator** remembers, reasons, retrieves, calculates, writes code, searches and acts.
- **The orchestrator never takes over the conversation.** It returns the substance, and the speech plane decides how that substance is spoken.

## Ratified decisions

| # | Decision (operator, 2026-09-24) |
|---|---|
| D1 | **The interlocutor is chosen by a bake-off.** Step-Audio 2 mini is the lead and Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B the challenger. Today's whisper+qwentts cascade is the baseline. The controller is model-agnostic, so the winner is a backend, not an architecture. |
| D1a | Quantizing the Qwen3-Omni Thinker to about Q4 is acceptable. The intelligence ceiling comes from the orchestrator, not the interlocutor. |
| D2 | **Languages.** English is mandatory. Italian is strongly wanted, and en+it is acceptable. German and French are desirable. Language is the **first** bake-off gate. |
| D3 | **Placement is decided by measurement.** PyTorch serves as a *measurement-only* reference. The operator leans towards the GPU. The decisive number is whether speech (cascade, then interlocutor) can co-reside on card 2 with 35B-A3B (the documented lean), or on card 1 with the 27B if VRAM allows, without either side missing its target. CPU speech placement is retained only as the CS-26 degraded fallback. (rescoped 2026-10-21 after operator ruling: frontdoor moves to a GPU with card 2). |
| D4 | **The production runtime is our own llama.cpp-family port**, built on an experimental tree and promoted as a new kernel version. vLLM and PyTorch are measurement instruments only, per the standing rule in `wiki/inference-serving.md` ("vLLM is a measurement instrument, not a second deployment engine"). |
| D5 | **Routing.** The controller sends every *substantive* turn to the orchestrator. Local answers are limited to social, backchannel and interaction-control turns. The operator will reassess model-decided routing once routing accuracy has been measured. |
| D6 | **A dedicated voice-turn contract** in the orchestrator: real token streaming, a session-keyed conversation store, a spoken-form answer plus an optional display payload with preserve/verbatim fields, and a voice routing policy. It should be whatever is simplest to implement that meets this. |
| D7 | **The client and transport are deferred.** Recorded end-state constraint: the operator will eventually talk to the machine *remotely*, from a travel laptop or a phone. Nothing may assume the microphone is on the host. |
| D8 | **The cascade is kept** as the baseline and as the permanent fallback / degraded mode. |
| D9 | **Second MI210.** It arrives within about a month. The operator's plan is Qwen3.6-35B-A3B on it, possibly co-hosting the speech plane. It may serve as the **bench** before 35B-A3B is commissioned. |
| D10 | **A custom or cloned persona voice is nice to have, not mandatory.** It is a tie-break criterion in the bake-off. Any cloning obeys the default-deny guardrail carried below. |
| D11 | **Latency hiding is a ladder, cheapest rung first:** native preamble, then streamed answer, then untrained mid-generation injection. KAME-style training of the front-end is a **gated research track**. |
| D12 | **This handoff is the one home for speech.** It is the conversation-stack handoff that KVU-11 anticipated, and it absorbs the open speech items from `multimodal-pipeline.md`. |

- [ ] **OD-A — confirm speech host card.** Default card 2 beside 35B-A3B, gated on CS-9 numbers; CS-26 cascade fallback kept. Operator confirms at CS-9 readout. (rescoped 2026-10-21 after operator ruling: frontdoor moves to a GPU with card 2)

## Accumulated insights — 2026-10-20 (for Phase 2 / D1 when the second MI210 is installed)

Added 2026-10-20 at the operator's request ("bake tonight's work into the handoff/vision so that we can pick it up when the time comes with our accumulated insights"). This section adds to the ratified Vision and D1–D8; it changes none of them and no task text. Every bullet points to the note, artifact or commit it came from.

**Turn detection (CS-22, CS-47)**
- CS-22's text calls semantic end-of-turn "optional" and names a silence threshold as the policy. The evidence below points toward a semantic-gated design (a probability or acoustic model decides complete vs incomplete, silence only gates it) as the candidate default. This is a design insight, not an edit to CS-22. Pointers: CS-47 findings (Smart Turn behind the Silero gate is the only arm that moved the false-cut rate); the Unmute reference, whose end-of-turn is a model pause head thresholded 0.6 / 0.4 rather than a silence timer (intake-2054#record); M10 (further end-of-turn models) in the durable triggers below.
- CS-47 synthetic results (CS-47 accuracy-legs note, research `artifacts/cs47_turn_end_bakeoff_20261020/`): the incomplete clips carry 2.0 s of trailing silence, so every silence-gated or acoustic arm fires on nearly all of them (silence 1.000; Parakeet-EOU 0.972 to 1.000, including the no-fallback rows). Smart Turn discriminates a little (gate 0.6 theta 0.9: false-cut difference -0.333 vs the nearest-latency silence row, paired CI [-0.515, -0.181]) but selection-only extension to FPR <= 0.10 costs recall <= 0.017. Parakeet-EOU ties its matched silence row at 0.000 difference. No arm meets the 0.10 FPR budget on selection; no arm advances.
- Adaptive-arm results (CS-47 addendum A, research `0d495b8fc`, offline re-score, synthetic stratum only; ranks and rejects, cannot advance): no semantic-gated adaptive variant yields a usable operating point. The only FPR-feasible Smart Turn + Silero point (theta 0.99, gate 0.4, T_short 0.5, T_long 2.5) has confirmation recall 0.296, EOT FPR 0.000, INT FPR 1.000 and reaches FPR 0 by never answering (100% of incomplete clips censored never-fired, 0% of complete clips answered within T_short); Parakeet-EOU adaptive equals its no-fallback rows (EOT FPR 1.000). The 2.0 s digital tail means any T_long >= 2.5 s arm cannot fire in-clip, so this stratum cannot test those arms (follow-up CS-45.1).
- Only the `human_recorded` stratum can advance an arm, and it is EMPTY (CS-4 sets 2 and 5 are not recorded): `corpus.json` keeps `advance_eligible_strata = [human_recorded]` (CS-45 note; `docs/reference/speech/turn-taking-measurands.md` §0 rule 3). Recording it is the gating input for every turn-end decision.
- Latency leg: the per-decision CPU cost half ran 2026-10-20 on a LOADED host (CS-47 addendum B, research `0d495b8fc` `latency_now/`; contended, one pass, no A/A pair): criterion (c) CPU p95 <= 100 ms PASSES for every arm at 1 core (worst 43.4 ms) and at 4 cores Parakeet fp32_fp32 160 ms (86.1 ms) is BORDERLINE and Smart Turn (79.1 ms) is at the edge; those two 4-core rows are queued for a quiet-host re-run with the staged script (`artifacts/cs47_turn_end_bakeoff_20261020/scripts/latency.sh`, CS-47 note). Signed latency in the accuracy legs is stream time of the first fire minus gold EOT with no compute lag added; model-only latency is separated from any wrapper gate (measurands §2.2).

**Full-duplex / interlocutor (D1, M8)**
- PersonaPlex / Moshi was marked BLOCKED only because llama.cpp lacks the Moshi architecture (`handoffs/archived/personaplex_voice_interface.md`, "Blocker" section). Under the operator's platform rule a missing port is a porting question, not a decline. It is English-only, so it fails the D2 language gate as a winner and can only be a full-duplex reference slot behind the CS-20 backend interface. The M8 trigger already scopes this (second MI210 installed, CS-5 harness exists, a reference requested for CS-8 or CS-28): PyTorch-ROCm offline smoke on one clip as a known-answer for the CS-48 scorers, plus a zero-compute ggml op-coverage note.
- MiniCPM-o 4.5 full-duplex streaming was never tested (`handoffs/archived/minicpm-o-4_5-integration.md`: full-duplex listed as "test if supported", next step 4).
- Full-Duplex-Bench v1 `pause_handling` is the existing instrument for the case where a user pauses mid-turn and the model must not take the floor (TOR lower-is-correct there; measurands §2.1). CS-48 carries the adapter.
- The D1 bake-off (Step-Audio 2 mini vs Qwen3-Omni, CS-8) has not run. A native full-duplex or omni interlocutor does its own turn-taking, which is an alternative to the CS-22 controller policy. Measure it with the same instruments as the cascade: the CS-45 corpus and measurands, the CS-48 barge-in harness, and the same denominators, so the two policies are comparable.

**Speech kernels**
- Thread study (CS-45 qwen-tts thread-scaling follow-up, `artifacts/qwentts_threads_20261020/`): `ggml_barrier` (`ggml-cpu.c:575`) is a pure `_mm_pause` spin and qwen-tts builds a disposable pool per graph compute, so one descheduled thread stalls all N. On a quiet region 8 threads is about 4x faster than 1 (38.3 vs 94.4 ms/frame); with 8 spinners on 8 pinned cpus it reaches 9754 ms/frame. The trigger is core sharing, not thread count. Recommendation recorded there: N threads only on cores nothing else runs on, otherwise one thread per process.
- Persistent-threadpool + non-spinning-barrier fix: **FOLDED into the speech champions 2026-10-21** (fast-forward, champion only, not production): `champion/speech-qwentts` @ `b1a1ec7` (carries the ggml pointer bump), `champion/speech-qwentts-ggml` @ `05e1ad6c`, `champion/speech-whisper` @ `fa5321e4`, all on the `fork` remotes. qwentts gate and timing PASS; whisper correctness and quiet PASS but its predeclared >=5x total-latency target is MISSED (2.4-2.5x; see the fold note under CS-45). Manifest rows SP2-005 / SP2-006 updated in the same commit.
- Speech champions exist on the forks: `champion/speech-qwentts` @ `2c1b5182e`, `champion/speech-qwentts-ggml` @ `b86f6602`, `champion/speech-whisper` @ `b30737922`. Release manifest and fold rules: [`docs/reference/kernel-manifests/speech-v2-manifest.md`](../../docs/reference/kernel-manifests/speech-v2-manifest.md).
- The whisper WER anchor is 3.37% (63/1870, large-v3-turbo f16 MI210 greedy), not the 2.35% in the ratification receipt, which is the faster-whisper int8 CPU arm (manifest SP2-001 and "At risk of being dropped" item 3; `measurement/protocols/speech.md` Appendix S-A). The superseding receipt is human-only.

**Instruments ready**
- CS-45 v2 corpus at research `cb6b08fea`: 112 gate-passing main pairs plus 27 reserve, 278 clips each bound to a passing independent-gate result; consumer-tool fix `beeb3a352`. Lessons (CS-45 notes): QA must be a human ear plus an independent ASR, because QA that reuses the corpus's own build tools is circular (v1 failed 12 of 20 sampled pairs while its own QA marked every cut clean); and the verifier and the consumer tool must share one assembly function (`tt_corpus.assemble_row()`), cross-tested, or the verifier can pass on code the consumer rejects.
- CS-48 barge-in harness: research `scripts/voice/barge_in/` (pushed @`10b1b591a`, final @`68db8b5c4`; 30 conformance tests, mutation cases refused). FDB v1.5 data fetched to `/mnt/raid0/llm/data/fdb-v1.5-20261020/`, 498 samples (200/100/100/98) loaded by `scenarios_from_fdb15`, not yet scored through the harness.
- CS-46 Parakeet-EOU ONNX arm: fp32 encoder + fp32 decoder_joint is step-exact with the NeMo CPU streaming run on 20 of 20 clips; the int8 decoder_joint is excluded (false `<EOU>` on CTX_BC `tt1-p0081-b`).
- CS-47 latency script: staged; the loaded-host CPU-cost run is recorded (addendum B), the quiet-host re-run of the two 4-core borderline rows is still owed (see Turn detection).

**Placement and contention**
- Speech on shared CPU cores collapses: the thread study above, and `docs/reference/speech/cpu-speech-contention-20260924.md` (the 2026-09-24 measurements behind option D and CS-12).
- GPU host threads take CPU cores, so the second card's host lane affects the CPU timing floor: CS-7 requires recording the CPU-floor impact and feeds INF-22 P2-5j (`gpu-serving-tie-in-program.md`). No number is recorded here.
- Scheduling rules: correctness work (builds, op tests, scoring, parity) can pair under build-role claims; timing work (any rate or latency comparison) needs an exclusive window. `agents/shared/OPERATING_CONSTRAINTS.md` → "Correctness work needs no quiet host; only timing does".

### When the second MI210 is installed — pickup checklist

Plain bullets, deliberately not checkboxes: the tasks they point to own the checkboxes.
- Verify the device and ROCm: two gfx90a agents, NUMA node, PCIe link, measured H2D/D2H bandwidth, host-thread lane and CPU-floor impact (CS-7).
- Phase 2: move whisper and qwentts onto the new card with a measured result, device-true registry and dashboard text, per-service `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, re-cut speech-VRAM reservation (CS-12; co-residency and CU-mask measurements in CS-9; CPU alternative in CS-11).
- Run the D1 interlocutor bake-off on it, cascade measured on the same clips as the baseline (CS-5 harness, CS-8 gates). Include a full-duplex reference only if M8's trigger holds; measure native turn-taking with the CS-45 / CS-48 instruments.
- Re-run the two borderline 4-core CS-47 latency rows on a quiet host (staged script; the 1-core PASS and the loaded-host numbers are already recorded) and CS-49 on the live cascade there (CS-21 services up in a window owned by the inference-owning session).
- Record the `human_recorded` stratum (CS-4 sets 2 and 5): without it no CS-47 arm can advance and CS-22's policy stays the silence default.
- DONE 2026-10-21: the persistent-threadpool / non-spinning-barrier fix was folded into the speech champions (SP2-005, SP2-006 updated in the same commit); the promotion of any speech kernel stays a significant-bundle decision for the operator.

## Facts that constrain the design (verified 2026-09-24)

**Host**
- **One MI210 today** (gfx90a, ROCm 6.2.0-66, NUMA node 1, PCIe Gen4 x16).
  - It holds :8083 Qwen3.8-27B np4/kvu (42.9 GB) and :8086 Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B (24.0 GB), with **0.9–1.9 GiB free** (contention doc §6; `wiki/inference-serving.md:138`).
  - Host threads use `GPU_HOST_LANE` 184-191, which is cross-node from the GPU (`heterogeneous-slot-fabric-residency.md:132`).
  - Nothing measured exists for PCIe H2D/D2H bandwidth.
- **The second MI210 (D9).** The planned occupant is Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-MTP Q8_0. Measured VRAM:

  | np | VRAM (GB) | tok/s |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 | 36.6 | 112.68 |
  | 4 | 37.5 | 189.57 aggregate |
  | 16 | 41.4 | 310.96 aggregate, still climbing |

  Estimated VRAM for the other co-tenants (verify in CS-9):

  | Co-tenant | VRAM |
  |---|---|
  | Cascade (whisper.cpp large-v3-turbo about 2.2–2.6 GB, plus qwentts about 1.2 GB) | ≈ 4 GB |
  | Step-Audio 2 mini: Q8 decoder, BF16 encoder, Token2Wav, 16k KV | ≈ 11–12 GB |
  | Step-Audio 2 mini at BF16 | ≈ 19–20 GB |
  | Qwen3-Omni with a Q4 Thinker, plus Talker, Code2Wav and AuT | ≈ 23 GB |
  | Qwen3-Omni with a Q8 Thinker | ≈ 36+ GB, which does **not** fit beside a Q8 35B-A3B |

- **CPU decode bandwidth.** Decode-usable bandwidth is about **150–220 GB/s**, not the 460.8 GB/s theoretical figure (`wiki/hardware-optimization.md:75-78,119,223`).
  - *Correction 2026-09-26:* 150–220 GB/s is pre-BIOS. Since 2026-09-21 a full-screen read-sum measures 446.8 GB/s at t96 (cpu-decode-roofline-program.md:282-289) and 449.4 under load (deepseek-v41-flash-evaluation.md:668-674, DS41-C36). A ~250 GB/s decoder is no longer excluded by the ceiling alone; whether it fits beside a -t 96 LLM is the contention question in the next bullet.
  - CPU speech **collapses on both sides** while a `-t 96` CPU LLM generates: STT RTF ≥ 58, TTS first packet 13.4–13.6 s, and the LLM falls to 0.59–1.05 tok/s (contention doc §4).
  - Partitioning the LLMs to `-t 56` on cores 40-95 restores STT, leaves TTS marginal, and costs the LLM 22–26%. **The frontdoor under that partition was never measured.**
- **No free physical core.** Even correctly pinned GPU host threads degraded the CPU A/A floor from 0.80% to 7.22%.

**Orchestrator (epyc-orchestrator @ fd4f49cc)**
- **No voice path exists.** STT and TTS are launched and health-checked (`scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:2678-2813`; `orchestration/launch_manifest.yaml:757-865`) but never called. There is no VAD, turn detection, barge-in, websocket or client.
- **`/v1/chat/completions` streaming is fake.** The answer is fully generated, then replayed character by character (`src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1211-1232`). Real llama-server chunks already exist in `src/llm_primitives/inference.py` `_call_caching_backend` (`on_chunk` closures at :1221/:1253/:1292), but they feed only the tap, the repetition guard and cancellation. `primitives.llm_call` (`src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:663`) returns `str`.
- **No conversation store.** `x_session_id` is "RECORDED ONLY" (`src/api/models/openai.py:192-202`). The session sqlite store has no messages table (`src/session/sqlite_store.py:123-257`). The trace store (`src/trace/navigation.py:117` `get_conversation`) is the closest thing.
- **The MCP `orchestrator_chat` tool wraps `/chat`** (prompt plus a context string), not `/v1` (`src/mcp_server.py:451-500`).
- **Voice routing to :8083 is unimplemented.** "Voice turns reason on the :8083 27B" (KVU-11; that role is `architect_critic` since ARCHSWAP-20260927) is written in docs only; no routing code implements it.

**Speech kernels today**
- Release manifest for the next speech kernel set: [`docs/reference/kernel-manifests/speech-v2-manifest.md`](../../docs/reference/kernel-manifests/speech-v2-manifest.md) (must-carry gfx90a patches, threadpool work status, proposed `champion/speech-*` branches). Edit it in the same commit as any speech-kernel change.
- **whisper.cpp** is `production-speech-v1` `b3073792` (ggml 0.18.0).
  - It serves `POST /v1/audio/transcriptions`: one shot, WAV only, no streaming. It pads to 30 s, so a 3.5 s utterance costs about 2 s on CPU and about 0.12 s on GPU.
  - Built-in Silero VAD flags exist but are unused.
  - Its WER is **3.37%** (n=100, CI [2.26, 4.67], Annex S Appendix S-A). This is **not** the 2.35% quoted in multimodal S-10, which was the faster-whisper CPU arm.
- **qwentts.cpp** is `2c1b518` (ggml 0.17.0).
  - It serves `POST /v1/audio/speech` and streams PCM (24 kHz s16 mono) by default.
  - Runtime voice registration exists; no voice is registered at start-up.
  - It supports 10 languages, including en/it/de/fr, **so the cascade already covers D2 in full**.
  - GPU figures at freeze: RTF 0.169, TTFA 37.8 ms, round-trip WER 1.49%.
  - v-next speech kernel: persistent threadpool + non-spinning barrier (`ggml_barrier` pure `_mm_pause` spin, per-compute pool create/free; 13x loss under core sharing). **Built and folded into the champions 2026-10-21**: `champion/speech-qwentts` @ `b1a1ec7`, `champion/speech-qwentts-ggml` @ `05e1ad6c`, `champion/speech-whisper` @ `fa5321e4` (qwentts timing PASS 11-32x under co-run; whisper total-latency target MISSED at 2.4-2.5x). Evidence and fold note in the CS-45 notes; not promoted.

**Candidates.** Primary sources were checked on 2026-09-24; URLs are in *Sources* below.

| | Step-Audio 2 mini | Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B-Instruct |
|---|---|---|
| Shape | 8.315B total. The AR core is a Qwen2.5-7B derivative, ~7.66B dense, 16,384 context. The audio encoder is ~0.64B (Qwen2-Audio). Token2Wav is ~1.23 GB (CosyVoice2-style flow DiT 512×16 plus HiFT and CAM++). | The Thinker is 30B-A3B MoE (~3B active). The Talker is 3B-A0.3B MoE plus an MTP module. Code2Wav is ~200M. AuT is ~650M. |
| Output clock | **1 text token : 4 audio tokens** (TA4) at 25 Hz, so **about 31.25 AR steps per second of speech** (StepFun maintainer: "~32 Hz", issue #42) | The Talker runs at 12.5 Hz with MTP. The theoretical first packet is 234 ms. |
| Input tokens | 12.5 tokens per second of audio. The official pattern re-feeds past turns, so 16k fills in **about 9–12 minutes** of conversation. | Not yet measured here (CS-8). |
| Speech output languages | **No evidence for it/de/fr.** Published evaluations are zh/en only, and English is weaker than Chinese (URO-Bench Pro 61.25 vs 69.57). | **en, zh, fr, de, ru, it, es, pt, ja, ko** (model card) |
| Tool calling | Only in the StepFun vLLM fork's parser. A call comes **only after `<tts_end>`**, as `<tts_start>[optional spoken preamble]<tts_end><tool_call>…`, so a tool use takes two generations. **No published tool-call accuracy for mini**; the table covers the full Step-Audio 2 only. Issue #27 reports poor slot filling. | Tools and RAG act on the Thinker's text output. The Talker voices whatever text it is given, so orchestrator text could go to the Talker directly. |
| Paralinguistics | StepEval-Paralinguistic average 80.00. That is 50 samples per dimension, LLM-judged, so each dimension carries about ±7–14 pp of noise. | Strong on published audio benchmarks; not compared on StepEval. |
| Voice | Zero-shot from a prompt wav via CAM++ and flow conditioning (tie-break per D10) | 3 fixed voices, no cloning |
| Duplex | Half-duplex. No VAD is shipped. | Half-duplex |
| Runtime status | **None on our hardware.** No llama.cpp support. `token2wav.py` hard-codes `.cuda()` (the CPU PR #30 was closed unmerged). Issue #86 shows only a server *starting* on gfx1151 with ROCm 7.11: no generation, no audio. vLLM-Omni ROCm is validated on gfx942 only. | Our llama.cpp supports Thinker audio input (open bug #27136 on the ggml-org GGUF). There is **no Talker or Code2Wav** in llama.cpp. vLLM-Omni is NVIDIA-only. |
| Published serving numbers | vLLM-Omni on 4×3090: TTFP 1437 ms, RTF 0.949 (async chunk). StepFun estimate on an L40S: about 1 s to first audio. | Not collected |
| Known issues | #75 repetition loops (open), #58/#33 system-prompt fragility, #83 text-only output | |
| License | Apache-2.0 | Apache-2.0 |

**Corrections to the operator draft.** They are recorded so nobody re-imports them.
1. There is one MI210 today, not two. Topology B needs D9.
2. There are 31.25 decode steps per second of speech, not 25. The bandwidth math in draft §8/§10 is 25% optimistic.
3. The 460 GB/s-class figure is theoretical. On this host, Q8 on CPU at 31.25 steps/s needs about 250 GB/s, which is unrealistic. Q4 needs about 140 GB/s and is plausible only on a quiet CPU.
4. Issue #86 is not a working ROCm inference.
5. The tool-call strength is proven for the full model, not mini.
6. The context must be managed from Phase 1 onward, not Phase 4.
7. The "existing chat endpoint" lacks streaming and memory.
8. The STITCH paper (arXiv 2507.15375) is Microsoft/NTU work, not Kyutai's.

---

## Start here

1. Read **Vision**, **Ratified decisions** and **Facts**. Do not re-open D1–D12.
2. The next actions do not need the second GPU. They are Phase 0 (CS-1…CS-6), Phase 3 (the orchestrator contract, CS-13…) and Phase 4 (the controller and cascade backend, CS-20…). **Phases 3 and 4 can be built end to end on today's cascade** before any new model exists.
3. When the second MI210 is physically installed, Phase 1 (the bench window) starts **before** 35B-A3B is commissioned (D9).

- [x] **CS-5.P1b — D1 load probe of Step-Audio-2-mini, and diagnosis of its audio incoherence.** ✅ 2026-10-23 — loaded (15.53 GiB, 15.67 peak) and text coherent (P3 "Paris"), but NOT coherent on audio (2026-10-22: P1_asr `ref_word_recall` 0.059, output `<英语>It's a little bit of a different sound.`; P2 "A person is speaking.", no content hits). Diagnosis by code reading, not yet confirmed on the GPU: the model's `prepare_inputs_for_generation` (`modeling_step_audio_2.py`) returns only `{input_ids, attention_mask, past_key_values}` and DROPS `wavs`/`wav_lens` whenever `kwargs["past_key_values"]` is not None. The repo pins transformers 4.49.0; transformers >= 4.56 (the container's; 4.57 source checked) always creates an empty `DynamicCache` for this class (`_supports_default_dynamic_cache` is true), so the FIRST step already takes the cache branch, the audio encoder never runs, and the LLM reads literal `<audio_patch>` embeddings. `forward()` ignores the cache and re-embeds the full `input_ids` every step, so audio must be passed on every step with no cache. The mel path (16 kHz asserted, 128 mels, padding 479) and the `<|BOT|>human` prompt format match the official `stepaudio2.py`; text works, which fits. Prepared fix in `d1_probe.py` (backup `.bak-pre-stepfix-20261023`): per-instance `prepare_inputs_for_generation` override that passes wavs every step, `use_cache=False`, an encoder forward-pre-hook recording `encoder_calls` (0 means the audio never ran), `transformers` version recorded, and a non-gating `P1b_asr_official` prompt (instruction in the system turn, as `examples.py`).

### Phase 4: voice controller and cascade backend (can start now)

- [ ] **CS-20 — build the voice-controller skeleton and the interlocutor backend interface** in `epyc-orchestrator` (proposed `src/voice/`).
  - Interface: `ingest(audio_chunk)`, `respond(turn) → stream of events {text_delta, audio_chunk, tool_call, end}`, `inject(text)`, `cancel()`, `health()`.
  - Test harness: WAV in → PCM/WAV out plus a JSONL event log, with no client (D7).

This is the first end-to-end voice path, the permanent fallback (D8), and the bake-off baseline. It exercises the whole controller and contract before any new model exists.

  - The production Silero weights must be pinned; the only copy on disk is a test fixture.

  Pick by precision and recall on the CS-4 routing set. Log every routing decision for CS-41.

Kernel rules apply throughout: work on an experimental branch pulled fresh from production; production trees are never modified; promote a full candidate.

  - Omni: verify AuT parity.

  - Step Token2Wav: UpsampleConformerEncoderV2, the flow DiT (512 wide, 16 deep, 10 ODE steps), HiFT, and CAM++ run once per enrolled voice. Stream in chunks of 25 tokens plus 3 lookahead, with a flow attention cache and HiFT crossfade.
  - Omni: the Talker (MoE plus MTP) and Code2Wav.

  - First prove the binary under test contains the port (a `strings` / mtime check, per the build-artifact rule).

### Phase 8: gated research

- [ ] **CS-41 — reassess model-decided routing (D5 → model-decided LOCAL answers).** Proceed only after CS-24 shows routing precision and recall at or above the operator-set target on the confirmation split. The operator decides.
- **[declined: ws58 2026-11-03 content-review: a training task, out of scope for this stack; opens only if CS-39 falls short, which is unmeasured]** **CS-42 — KAME-style oracle-stream fine-tune of the front-end.** Open this only after CS-39 shows untrained injection falls short on perceived latency or fidelity.
  - Feasibility, estimated 2026-09-24 and unmeasured:
    - LoRA on an 8B model fits one MI210 (BF16 weights 16.6 GB plus gradient checkpointing).
    - A full fine-tune (≈16 B/param ≈ 130 GB plus activations) needs two MI210s with ZeRO-3/FSDP, or CPU optimizer offload (1.1 TB RAM).
    - About 85M tokens per epoch (56k dialogues × ~1.5k tokens) ≈ 4e18 FLOPs. At 30–40% of the MI210's 181 BF16 TFLOPS that is **≈15–20 h per epoch per GPU**.
    - Generating hint targets at six levels per dialogue is about 340k short orchestrator calls, around 2 days.
  - **The binding risk is data realism, not compute.** Synthetic TTS training speech can erode the paralinguistic perception the interlocutor was chosen for, so every run needs a CS-4 paralinguistic regression guard and a mix of real expressive speech.
  - Format: Step is single-stream, so the oracle becomes injected segments at TA4 boundaries rather than KAME's fourth stream. It needs the CS-34 runtime.
  - Prior art: intake-511#record (KAME) and SHANKS (arXiv 2510.06917, intake pending).

## Turn-taking evidence program — research intake 2026-10-20 (CS-22 / CS-23 instruments)

Research intake of the full-duplex / turn-taking sources (intake-2003#record, intake-2013#record, intake-2016#record, intake-2017#record, intake-2018#record, intake-2031#record, intake-2034#record, intake-2035#record, intake-2036#record, intake-2047#record, intake-2049#record, intake-2051#record, intake-2054#record, intake-2055#record, intake-2059#record, intake-2060#record and the related wave-1..6 entries). These tasks add the measurement instruments and the two pre-declared bake-offs that CS-22 and CS-23 need. They do not close CS-22 or CS-23 (the controller implementation stays there), they never modify the frozen speech kernels, and the speech-tree and production llama.cpp trees are untouched. Dependency order: CS-45 -> CS-46 -> CS-47, and CS-45 -> CS-48 -> CS-49 (CS-49 also needs the CS-21 live services). After CS-45 the CS-46/47 lane and the CS-48/49 lane run concurrently. CS-2 consumes the CS-45 measurand note when its Annex S package is prepared (human-only boundary; no package text is written here).

talking-to-others and background-speech overlap classes; and a self-correction (mid-utterance intent change) class annotated now, its tool-call scoring left to CS-24/CS-36; (b) measurand note `docs/reference/speech/turn-taking-measurands.md`, which CS-2 consumes when its package is prepared: FDB v1/v1.5 TOR, smooth-turn latency, t_stop and t_resp with scenario-dependent direction (a HIGH t_stop is correct for backchannel / off-talk / background speech), TurnBench EOT recall / FPR / signed latency and INT FPR, ECHO PASR / PKC read together with the constant-policy baselines, HumDial first-response delay and timing-rejection rule; FDB v1 backchannel JSD excluded; (c) a paired minimal-contrast set of at least 100 English pairs built by splicing ONE recording (identical waveform; only the cut point or the preceding assistant context differs; matched trailing silence) so pairs are acoustically matched, qwentts-synthesised and human-recorded strata kept separate, and CS-4 set 2/5 hesitation and backchannel clips joining the manifest as they land; (d) external cross-check material held OUTSIDE the Annex S corpus as hashes and ids only: the TurnBench dev split (execution condition: the operator supplies an HF token and accepts the gated terms), optional ECHO (gated HF request) and optional HumDial Track-2 train/dev (registration email); an absent set changes nothing else in this program; (e) one instrument pin set: a single pinned Silero VAD (sha256 recorded; the faster_whisper-asset `silero_vad_v6.onnx` or a fresh pinned download), Full-Duplex-Bench commit 3e799c4, the TurnBench baseline commit 38a6f87, and the ASR used for any word-timestamp metric, with scorer scripts patched to read the pinned local files (no torch.hub fetch, no `.cuda()`) and every substitution recorded as a measurement-era change. The independent QA gates (U1, U2, U3, A1 plus `tt_verify` check 13) are therefore the acceptance criterion for the rebuilt corpus; no fresh review packet was produced and the v1 packet is archived (`/mnt/raid0/llm/datasets/turntaking-cs45-v1/review_packet_v1_superseded`).
    - **v1 (`fe730c481`) was defective and is superseded.** Operator review: 12 of 20 sampled pairs bad (5/10 CUT_IC cut about one word off, 1/4 CUT_WAIT silent, 6/6 CTX_BC assistant stopped mid-utterance) although "clean cut" was marked Y everywhere. Root causes with file:line evidence: `qa_evidence_pre_fix/root_causes.json` (whisper.cpp heuristic timestamps with no DTW, 1737 of 1737 words `dtw_ms = null`, plus a deepest-valley cut in a +-160 ms window; an absolute -50 dB trim that counted a -42 dBFS noise lead as speech; the continuing assistant truncated at `user_end + TRAIL`). The v1 QA was circular: the ASR that placed the cut also judged it. The new gates, run on all v1 clips, flagged 96 of 130 pairs (all 12 human-rejected ones plus p0012/p0013/p0028, which the ear had passed).
    - **v2 corpus (what is committed now).** 112 main English pairs, each ONE spliced recording (CUT_IC 69, CUT_WAIT 13, CTX_BC 30; selection/confirmation disjoint by speaker, dialogue, source recording and assistant context) plus 27 reserve pairs; all 278 clips carry a passing independent-gate result bound to their PCM-SHA-256 (`qa/qa_gates_report.json`). Synthesized with the frozen qwentts `production-speech-v1` CLI on CPU (read-only; ggml linkage proved; 139 of 139 render logs show the CPU backend). Cut placement: ASR-A = faster-whisper large-v3-turbo generate-and-test (prefix and suffix must transcribe exactly, deepest energy minimum); gates: ASR-B = faster-whisper large-v3 (different weights from both ASR-A and the scoring ASR whisper.cpp large-v3-turbo). CTX_BC: the continuing assistant now runs to its natural end with a per-pair tail (>= 3 s past the backchannel, ends >= 0.3 s before the clip does). 37 of 176 candidate pairs (146 main incl. 36 alternate sentences, 30 reserve) had no variant that passed and are simply absent (`qa/selection_report.json`); nothing was waived. `corpus.json` sha256 `c784e3b033c5f51f7189d7c58fbd5c313d3802bfb59b1492a882a7c97541be25`.

    - Findings carried forward: (1) `qwen-tts` under host load runs 472 ms/frame at 8 threads vs 36 ms/frame at 1 thread, so synthesis should use parallel single-thread processes. (2) The repo PII pre-commit hook text-scans staged blobs under 1 MB, so audio is stored in tar archives. (3) Whisper-family ASR hallucinates a plausible continuation (or "thank you for watching") after any truncated utterance and completes a cut word, so an exact-transcript gate on a cut clip is deliberately strict; sentences whose next word is highly predictable fail every render variant ("about to ___", "some extra ___") and were dropped, not waived. (4) CS-48 consumes only channel 0 of the CTX_BC clips, so the assistant truncation never reached its stimulus; its CS-45 binding is re-checked against `cb6b08fea`.
    - **2026-10-20 defect found by CS-48/CS-47 and fixed (tick kept; corpus bytes and hashes unchanged, `corpus.json` sha256 still `c784e3b0...be25`).** On `cb6b08fea`, `tt_materialize.py` aborted with "PCM-SHA-256 mismatch" on CTX_BC clips (`tt1-p0081-a`, `-p0090-a`, `-p0099-a`) while `tt_verify.py` passed. Cause: v2 gave the context pairs a per-pair `layout.tail`, `tt_verify` passed it to `assemble_clip` but `tt_materialize` did not (the committed manifest and the committed tars were consistent). Fix, research `beeb3a352`: one shared `tt_corpus.assemble_row()` builds a clip from its manifest row for both tools, and `tt_materialize.py --include-reserve` covers main and reserve; no data file changed. New tests materialise EVERY committed clip like a consumer and fail if `tt_materialize` and `tt_verify` ever disagree (stale tar, stale layout tail). From a clean detached checkout of `beeb3a352` (`TT_PINS_DIR`/`CS45_DATA` unset): `tt_materialize` 278/278 clips verified, `tt_verify` 13 checks PASS, pytest 82 passed. Lesson: the verifier and the consumer tool must share one assembly function and be cross-tested; a verifier that is only run next to its own builder can pass on code its consumers do not use.

    - **2026-10-20 qwen-tts thread-scaling follow-up (CS-45 finding (1); factual, nothing changed; evidence `artifacts/qwentts_threads_20261020/`).** Frozen `/mnt/raid0/llm/qwentts.cpp/build/qwen-tts` (ggml 0.17.0, pthread pool; `libggml-cpu.so` has no OpenMP/KMP/GOMP strings; linkage PASS), CS-45 model/codec/ref-wav/text, `--greedy --seed 1234 --max-new 24` (24 frames every run), threads via the CS-45 `nprocs_shim` (CLI has no thread flag; default is `hardware_concurrency()/2`), CPU only, under `region-lock` on q1 (cpus 24-47, quiet: region busy 3-24% = our own threads; host loadavg 43-47 from other regions). Total ms/frame (talker + code predictor), median [min-max], 5 reps. **Quiet region, pinned to N cpus / free over 24 cpus:** 1T 94.4 [94.1-97.6] / 96.2; 2T 56.9 / 64.2; 4T 45.8 / 34.8; 8T 38.3 [37.3-41.2] / 23.9 [22.9-28.0]; 16T 41.8 [31.2-44.3] / 21.4 [20.6-22.3]. (Talker-only 1T 33.4, 8T free 8.8 ms/frame; CS-45's 36 ms/frame at 1T matches the talker-only figure.) So on a quiet region 8 threads is about 4x FASTER than 1, and the 13x collapse is NOT a property of thread count. **Induced contention with our own captured spinner PIDs (3 reps, Total ms/frame):** 1T sharing its core with 1 spinner 189; 1T free with 24 spinners 222; 2T pinned + 2 spinners 457; 8T pinned + 1 spinner on one of its cpus 1221 [901-1485]; 8T free over 24 cpus + 24 spinners 5392; 8T pinned + 8 spinners 9754 [9453-10314] (talker 2840 ms/frame). **perf (own PID, 8T):** quiet 5.0% in `ggml_graph_compute_thread`, 27% `ggml_vec_dot_f16`; with 8 spinners 73.5% `ggml_graph_compute_thread` + 24.8% `ggml_compute_forward_mul_mat`, i.e. almost all samples are spinning. **Verdict: hypothesis VERIFIED in mechanism, with the trigger being core sharing, not thread count.** `ggml_barrier` (`ggml-cpu.c:575`) is a pure `_mm_pause` spin with no yield, and `qwen-tts` creates no persistent threadpool so each graph compute builds and frees a disposable pool; one descheduled thread stalls all N at every op barrier and the spinners burn the neighbours' cores. Not applicable (verified absent): OMP_PROC_BIND/OMP_PLACES/OMP_WAIT_POLICY (no OpenMP in the lib; 8T results with them set equal the unset ones: 23.8 free / 42.3 pinned), and no env poll knob exists (poll is only the `ggml_threadpool_params.poll` API field, and the binary does not use it). Not tested: the exact CS-45 background load, and whether CS-45's 8T used 8 truly free cores. **Recommendation (speech roles are frozen, nothing changed):** N threads only on cores nothing else runs on (region-claimed, SMT siblings free, or isolated); on shared or loaded cores use 1 thread per process, as CS-45 does. Pinning to N cpus was slower than a free 24-cpu mask when quiet (8T 38 vs 24), so do not pin tightly; leave slack cores for the scheduler. For the live :9002 service, an N>1 setting is only safe in a claimed region; a persistent threadpool with poll 0 in the speech kernel would be the real fix and needs a v-next kernel. **Update 2026-10-21: that fix now exists and is folded into the speech champions (`champion/speech-qwentts` @ `b1a1ec7`, `champion/speech-qwentts-ggml` @ `05e1ad6c`, `champion/speech-whisper` @ `fa5321e4`); see the fold note below. The recommendation above (1 thread per process on shared cores) still holds for the FROZEN production kernels.**
    - **2026-10-20 qwen-tts / whisper.cpp threadpool + non-spinning barrier fix on experimental branches (agent `qwentts-threadpool`; operator directive 2026-10-20; factual; production kernels untouched; NOT promoted; evidence `artifacts/qwentts_threadpool_20261020/`, start at `timing/timing_summary.md`).** Branches (all pushed to the `fork` remotes), each cut from `production-speech-v1`, gfx90a/ROCm 6.2 patches kept (G-ANC `b86f6602` and `b3073792` hold, FP8 guard `60300000` present): `qwentts.cpp-experimental-threadpool-20261020` @`b1a1ec7` (parent `2c1b518`), ggml submodule branch `ggml-experimental-threadpool-20261020` @`05e1ad6c` (parent `b86f6602`), `whisper.cpp-experimental-threadpool-20261020` @`fa5321e4` (parent `b3073792`); worktrees `/mnt/raid0/llm/worktrees/{qwentts,whisper}-threadpool-20261020`, candidate builds in their `build2/` (qwen-tts sha256 `5a22dd06...`, whisper-cli `6a0538f7...`; linkage PASS with each tree's own LD_LIBRARY_PATH, `candidate_proof.txt`). Diff: (1) qwentts `src/backend.h` creates ONE `ggml_threadpool` per CPU backend (via the registry proc addresses, poll 0 so idle workers sleep, paused=false, strict_cpu=false, no pinning) attached with `ggml_backend_cpu_set_threadpool`, freed after the backend in `backend_release`; `QT_THREADPOOL=0` restores the disposable pool; `qwen-tts --threads N` / `QT_CPU_THREADS` expose the count, default unchanged (`hardware_concurrency()/2`). whisper `src/whisper.cpp` does the same lazily per CPU backend in `ggml_graph_compute_helper(sched,...)` (pool sized to the requested `-t`, rebuilt if it changes, freed in `whisper_backend_free`; `WHISPER_THREADPOOL=0` opt-out). (2) `ggml-cpu.c` (identical ggml-cpu barrier code in 0.17.0 and 0.18.0, so one patch): `GGML_CPU_BARRIER=futex` (default on Linux) spins a bounded budget then parks in a futex wait on `n_barrier_passed` (last arriver wakes sleepers only if a counter says there are any), `yield` and `spin` (= stock, pure `_mm_pause`) selectable; budget `GGML_CPU_BARRIER_SPIN` = 2000 `_mm_pause` iterations; adaptive per waiting thread (EMA of 'barrier outlasted the budget': >25% -> `GGML_CPU_BARRIER_SPIN_LO` = 200, <3% -> 2000, re-probe every 8192; `GGML_CPU_BARRIER_ADAPT=0` fixes the budget). Why adaptive: fixed 2000 gave only 3.9x at 8T + 1 spinner (204 ms/frame), fixed <=800 gave 6-13x there but regressed the quiet 8T-free case 20-50%; adaptive meets both. Semantics read from ggml 0.17.0: `poll` = 1024*128*poll `_mm_pause` rounds of busy-polling for new work before the condvar sleep; `paused` starts workers parked; `strict_cpu` only matters with a cpumask. No change to the production trees. `GGML_CPU_BARRIER=spin QT_THREADPOOL=0` reproduces stock behaviour. Persistence proof (pthread_create counter shim): qwen-tts 24 frames 8T production 373 calls vs candidate 9; whisper-cli production 86 vs candidate 44 (7 pool threads plus whisper's own per-call `std::thread` in the mel front-end and the per-token beam-search sampler, which are not spin barriers and are NOT changed: follow-on).
      - **(a) Correctness: PASS (hard gate).** 10 fixed CS-45 texts (`texts/texts10.json`), CS-45 talker/codec/ref-wav, `--greedy --seed 1234 --max-new 40`, CPU only. Quiet: 1T 89.5 [85.5-91.4] vs 93.3 [89.7-95.6]; 8T free over the region 25.8 [24.4-27.4] vs 23.6 [23.2-27.4]; 8T pinned 40.1 [36.4-50.4] vs 35.5 [35.3-38.4]; production A/A (same binary, later block): 97.6 / 24.6 / 40.0, so the A/A drift is 9% at 1T, 4.7% free, 0.1% pinned and the candidate is inside it (predeclared: no regression beyond A/A noise: MET). Contended (predeclared >=5x at 8T): 8T pinned + 1 busy-loop spinner 715.6 [613.7-777.6] vs 63.6 [60.2-65.1] = 11.3x (pool alone, stock barrier: 580.3 = 1.2x, so the barrier is the fix); 8T + 8 spinners (3 reps) 9984 [9666-11255] vs 495 [479-513] = 20x; 8T + a real llama-bench neighbour (Qwen3-1.7B Q8_0, 8 threads, same 8 cpus, 3 reps) 13380 [13338-13421, n=2] vs 424 [417-425] = 31.6x, and the NEIGHBOUR's own throughput during the window rose from 0.35 tok/s (production speech) to 9.9 tok/s (candidate), alone 27.5 (it loses 64% instead of 99%); 1T + 1 spinner 197.0 vs 185.9 (n=7) = 1.06x and 1T + 1 llama-bench thread 189.5 vs 179.0 (neighbour 5.96 vs 6.17 tok/s of 14.4 alone): no barrier at 1T, so nothing to fix. Predeclared >=5x at 8T: MET (11.3x / 20x / 31.6x); neighbour loses no more than today: MET. whisper-cli (large-v3-turbo, 5.4 s clip padded to the 30 s window, 8T, 3 reps, 2 for 8 spinners), total ms: quiet free 4623 vs 4372 (1.06x); 1 spinner 13842 vs 5666 (2.44x); real neighbour 32180 vs 12900 (2.49x; neighbour 0.08 -> 2.04 tok/s of 27.5 alone); 8 spinners 30073 vs 12710 (2.37x). The >=5x target is MISSED on whisper TOTAL latency, because the encoder (~85% of the time, large matmuls, few barriers) is compute-shared and only gains 1.1-1.3x; the barrier-bound batched-decode phase gains 6915 -> 206 ms (33x), 14032 -> 1080 (13x), 14461 -> 1061 (13.6x), quiet 143 -> 137. whisper 1T and pinned legs, a whisper A/A block and the `exp_default` label (its `exp_futex` label is the explicit default env) were not run.

- [x] **CS-46 — build the Parakeet-Realtime-EOU-120M onnxruntime CPU arm and its parity gate (enabling for CS-47).** (a) Metadata-only load of the community ONNX graphs (`ysdede/parakeet-realtime-eou-120m-v1-onnx` @6d6be8e, encoder + fused decoder_joint): record input/output names, shapes and cache layout and diff them against the parakeet-rs @6ad1501 contract (encoder cache_last_channel [17,1,70,512], cache_last_time [17,1,512,8], decoder/joint states [1,1,640]); (b) a numpy 128-mel frontend from the parakeet-rs constants (16 kHz, n_fft 512, win 400, hop 160, pre-emphasis 0.97, natural log with additive guard), featurising only NEW frames per chunk (do not copy parakeet-rs's whole-buffer re-STFT), with its output diffed against the NeMo preprocessor on the manifest clips (this settles whether NeMo's EOU config needs per-feature normalisation or dither, which the Rust port omits); (c) a chunked greedy RNNT loop (chunk size a parameter: 80 ms NeMo-style, 160 ms parakeet-rs-style; 25-frame encoder slice = 9 pre-encode cache + 16 new; first output withheld until 1 s is buffered, as a parameter) that SURFACES the `<EOU>` event explicitly (in parakeet-rs, with reset_on_eou=false the token only breaks the decode loop and nothing is emitted) and runs two reset policies as variants: decoder-only reset (zero the decoder LSTM state, last_token=blank, keep encoder cache; parakeet-rs) vs full reset (audio buffer, encoder caches and hypotheses; NeMo voice agent); (d) fp32 encoder + fp32 decoder_joint, and fp32 encoder + int8 decoder_joint (the only int8 pairing the card calls exact, on one smoke fixture); encoder int8 is excluded as non-exact; (e) parity gate: token parity and EOU frame index against a NeMo CPU streaming run of `nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1` (NeMo >= 2.5.3 in an isolated CPU venv) on at least 10 CS-45 manifest clips, with a parakeet-rs run as secondary comparator; if NeMo cannot be installed on CPU the arm is labelled unvalidated and cannot advance past screening in CS-47; if the community graphs prove unusable, self-export with parakeet-rs `scripts/export_realtime_eou_120m.py` from the .nemo (the soniqo export is not used). All arms share one interface `feed(pcm_chunk) -> events[{t, kind}]`. Acceptance: parity report with per-clip raw token streams and feature diffs; the arm runs under a `region-lock` build-role claim (correctness work, no quiet host needed); weights fetched sequentially, never racing another download. ✅ 2026-10-20
  - **2026-10-20 execution note (cs46-exec; checkbox NOT ticked, awaiting main review):** built and pushed to `epyc-inference-research` main @`311898dcd` under `scripts/speech/parakeet_eou/` (usage and reproduction steps in its `__init__.py`); evidence under `artifacts/cs46_parakeet_eou_20261020/` (`results_summary.json` first; every number below is read from it or from the per-clip reports). Weights: community ONNX @6d6be8e (sha256 in `results_summary.json`), `nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1` HF commit `a7e2b462` (.nemo sha256 `6603a22a...9893`); NeMo 3.0.0 (>= 2.5.3 holds) + torch 2.14.1+cpu in an isolated venv under `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/cs-46-20261020/venv-nemo`; every CPU step ran under a `region-lock run --role build` claim; downloads sequential. The parakeet-rs clone is at `6ad1501` (the dispatch text's `2b08bdbe...` does not exist in it).
    - (a) **The community encoder graph is stateless**: inputs `audio_signal`,`length` only, no cache tensors, so the chunked cache-aware loop cannot run on it (`io_contract_diff.json`). Its `decoder_joint` has no `target_length`, takes `input_states_*` as `[1,batch,640]`, and returns log-softmax `[B,T,2,1027]` because the graph prepends an SOS step to `targets` on every call; carrying its state across calls double-counts SOS (measured: logits diverge from the step-wise export after the first token; slot 0 equals it only at the blank start). It is usable only in a "history" mode (committed tokens since the last reset, zero state, last slot), which the engine implements. Per the task text's fallback the cache-aware encoders were SELF-EXPORTED from the .nemo (`export_streaming_models.py`, parakeet-rs recipe, traced at 25 frames): a 160 ms encoder (valid_out_len 2) and an 80 ms voice-agent encoder (shift 1, cache_drop_size 1, valid_out_len 1) because both constants are baked into the graph, plus a step-wise fp32 decoder_joint; their I/O names and shapes match the parakeet-rs contract exactly (cache_last_channel [17,1,70,512], cache_last_time [17,1,512,8], states [1,1,640]) except one extra `encoded_lengths` output. int8 decoder_joint = the community int8 graph (history mode) and a dynamic-QInt8 copy of the self-exported decoder; encoder int8 excluded.
    - (b) Frontend `frontend.py`, new-frames-only (`StreamingMel`, block-size independent, equals whole-clip features to <= 2e-6 in the unit tests). Against NeMo's own preprocessor (eval mode) on 20 CS-45 clips the **Slaney** mel (librosa/NeMo) gives max |diff| 2.3e-4 (mean 2.8e-7, frame counts equal); the **HTK** filterbank that parakeet-rs `create_mel_filterbank_htk` builds gives max 14.3, mean 0.357 on the same clips, i.e. the Rust port's filterbank is not NeMo's. NeMo's model config has `normalize: NA` and `dither: 1e-5`: no per-feature normalisation, and dither only in training mode, so neither is needed. But NeMo's voice-agent `CacheFeatureBufferer` builds its preprocessor fresh and never calls `.eval()`, so the shipped service featurises with dither on (rolling 25-frame buffer vs our deterministic emulation: mean 0.0076, max 0.29 at 160 ms, 0.39 at 80 ms; against the same service with `.eval()` max 2.3e-4). The emitted token streams of the dithered and the eval-mode NeMo runs were identical on all 20 clips in all four (chunk, reset) configurations, so the gate uses the eval-mode run as the deterministic reference and reports the shipped one too.
    - (c) `engine.py`: `feed(pcm) -> [{t, kind, step, enc_frame, frame_t, id, piece}]`, kinds `token|eou|eob|reset`; `<EOU>` is surfaced under every reset policy (parakeet-rs with reset_on_eou=false emits nothing: confirmed natively, 0 of 20 clips show a `[EOU]` marker in the r0 runs). Chunk 80/160 ms; 25-frame rolling feature buffer (9 cache + 16 new at 160 ms; 9 + 8 new + 8 look-ahead at 80 ms), initial fill -16.635; `hold_s` queues events and releases them with `released_t` (decoding is never skipped); reset `none|decoder|full` (decoder = zero LSTM state + last=blank keep encoder cache and features; full = feature/sample buffers, encoder caches, decoder, hypotheses, applied after the step that held the EOU/EOB, as the NeMo service does). Two frontends: `nemo_va` (replicates the service's per-chunk window re-featurisation) and `exact` (new frames only, edge-correct). 13 unit tests pass.
    - (e) Parity vs the NeMo CPU streaming service (eval-mode frontend), 20 CS-45 manifest clips (CUT_IC 10, CTX_BC 6, CUT_WAIT 4; all synthesized, so none can advance an arm), `nemo_va` frontend, fp32 encoder + fp32 decoder_joint (community history mode AND self-exported): **20/20 clips step-exact (identical per-step token-id lists, hence identical EOU step) at 160 ms and 80 ms, reset none and full**. With the `exact` frontend the token streams are 20/20 identical and the EOU step 19/20, but per-step landing is exact on only 14-17/20 clips in `none` and 1-2/20 in `full` (NeMo's rolling window distorts the edge frames and a full reset restarts it). Against the shipped dithered NeMo run the same configs give token parity 20/20, step parity 17-20/20. fp32 encoder + int8 decoder_joint: community int8 token parity 19/20 (160 ms) and 18/20 (80 ms), EOU step 17/20; the dynamic-QInt8 copy 19/20 at both; **the failure that matters: on CTX_BC clip `tt1-p0081-b` (backchannel, NeMo emits no EOU) both int8 decoders emit a false `<EOU>`** (step 28 at 160 ms for the community graph, 25 for the copy), so int8 is not "exact" on this fixture set; the 80 ms community-int8 run also differs on `tt1-p0001-a` (edit distance 2, EOU step equal). Secondary comparator: native parakeet-rs @6ad1501 built with rustc 1.99.1 / ort 2.0.0-rc.15 (scratch example `eou_dump`, ORT CPU, 160 ms, self-exported 160 ms encoder + step-wise decoder, a `tokenizer.json` built from `vocab.txt`; text-only output): normalised text equals NeMo's on 14/20 clips, first-EOU call equal on 10 of the 15 clips where both fire (deltas -1..+1), as run with the HTK filterbank. The Rust port was not used as the primary reference.
    - Per-clip raw token streams, feature diffs, rows, configs and the event-stream samples (hold 1.0 s, three reset policies) are in the artifacts dir; the self-exported graphs (459 MB each) are not in git: `export_streaming_models.py` regenerates them in about 15 s on CPU (sha256 in `results_summary.json`), and the working copies are in `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/cs-46-20261020/export/` until that scratch is cleaned. No timing claims are made. Remaining to tick: main's review; every done criterion in the task text has an artifact.

- [ ] **CS-47 — run the CS-22 turn-end arm bake-off on EPYC CPU, with metrics and decision rule pre-declared in a run-plan commit before the first scored run.** Arms (every row tagged with weights revision, wrapper/gate, and threshold origin): (1) Silero + silence baseline: the pinned standalone Silero with silence thresholds {0.3, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.2} s, the NeMo voice-agent defaults (confidence 0.6, start 0.1 s, stop 1.2 s) as a named reference row, and whisper.cpp's built-in Silero flags if they expose usable timing; (2) Parakeet-EOU from CS-46 (both pairings, both reset policies, with the 1.2 s silence fallback); a missing or parity-failed arm is recorded as not run, in the denominator; (3) Smart Turn v3.2 int8 ONNX behind the Silero gate (onnxruntime CPUExecutionProvider, ORT_SEQUENTIAL, inter_op 1, WhisperFeatureExtractor time included) at the TurnBench published operating point (1 s silence gate, swept theta_eot 0.0082; 0.05 is only the wrapper's fallback default) AND at shortened gates; (4) VAP stereo (user channel + our TTS output channel): the public pretrained example checkpoint as the control row, and `oto.ckpt` from pinned HF revision b9aa0ba1 (sha256 recorded) as the second row, context length swept upward from 1 s; (5) probability-source arms also run Unmute's end 0.6 / barge 0.4 hysteresis as a named reference row, re-swept because those constants are calibrated to Kyutai's smoothed pause head, and Badcat's post-VAD hold (start 0.64 s) as a swept parameter. Scoring: TurnBench-format predictions.json adapters scored with `turnbench.score` (local CPU, no judge), plus paired yield/keep PASR with always-end and never-end rows, on the CS-45 manifest selection/confirmation split and on TurnBench dev when supplied; operating points are chosen on selection only (highest EOT recall at FPR <= 0.10) and the confirmation split is touched once. Pre-declared metrics: EOT recall; EOT FPR (false cut on a mid-turn pause); INT FPR (backchannel); signed latency p10/p50/p90 with model-only latency separated from any wrapper gate; PASR; per-decision CPU latency p50/p95 including featurisation over >= 30 repeats with A/A dispersion at the declared pinned core count and at 1 core. Denominator: every manifest item; crashes, timeouts and never-fire all count as misses. Decision rule (proposed thresholds, the operator may amend before filing): an arm ADVANCES to a CS-22 controller shadow arm iff on a human-recorded confirmation stratum it (a) has a lower false-cut rate than the silence row at matched median latency (arm <= that row + 100 ms), paired cluster-bootstrap 95% CI excluding zero, (b) has PASR above both constant-policy rows, and (c) has CPU p95 per decision <= 100 ms (10% of the proposed 1.0 s LOCAL budget); synthetic-only evidence can reject or rank an arm, never advance it; if no arm advances, Silero + silence stays and the M10 triggers are checked. TurnBench published rows (SmartTurn v3 = v3.1 weights + 1 s gate; VAP = fine-tuned checkpoint, 20 s windows, dev-swept thresholds) and the VAP paper's Table 1 weighted F1 are quoted only as tagged reference rows, never mixed with our measured rows or with each other. Timing legs run in an exclusive CPU window under `region-lock run --cpu-list`; accuracy legs run under build-role claims; every record is emitted through the VB-SPEECH-CONV-1 write side. Acceptance: arm table with per-item raw outputs (JSONL) and the decision-rule verdict filed; CS-22's open text gains the chosen policy or the recorded no-advance result.

Rule: at silence onset Smart Turn p (decision taken at gate 0.2 s, or 0.4 s when T_short 0.5) >= theta fires after T_short in {0.3, 0.5}; otherwise wait T_long in {2.5, 3, 5} s or until speech resumes; theta in {0.0082 ... 0.99}. Parakeet: the `<EOU>` token is the complete signal, no fixed fallback below T_long. Chosen on SELECTION only; confirmation here is a SECOND scorer touch (new arm family; the first touch was the main table). T_long > 2.0 s never fires inside a clip's 2.0 s tail, so a low-p incomplete clip is reported as never-fired/CENSORED, not as a correct hold.

INT FPR is 1.000 on confirmation (backchannel clips fire through the high-p path). Net: a degenerate never-fire point, not a usable arm. CTX_BC check: the CS-45 fix `beeb3a352` confirms the CTX_BC hash failure was `tt_materialize.py` omitting `layout.tail` (corpus bytes and manifest hashes UNCHANGED), and all 224 clips used here were hash-verified by `materialize.py`, so the CTX_BC rows stand.

Parakeet EOU fires on the 2.0 s digital silence regardless of semantics, so it holds nothing here.

It does not show the production practice fails; it shows Smart Turn v3.2 probabilities do not separate these TTS-cut incompletes from completes.

### Durable triggers — records, not active tasks (M12 .. M23)

- **M12 — STT runner and model upstream watch (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers, any one: (a) a Parakeet-family runner publishes gfx90a/ROCm validation, a ggml bump or GPU-kernel fixes that would close a CS-8.STT4 op gap (re-run that runner's CS-8.STT4 leg); (b) NVIDIA publishes a multilingual unified (offline plus streaming) checkpoint covering it/de/fr (add it as a CS-8.STT5 arm); (c) the ultra GGUF fails CS-8.STT4 parity on every runner (then the HF-to-NeMo ultra adapter, est. 120-200 LOC); (d) CS-20 needs multi-session STT (then exact batched decode in parakeet.cpp); (e) an independent CrispASR whisper-parity proof or WER reproduction appears. Sources: intake-2344#record, intake-2347#record, intake-2348#record, intake-2349#record, intake-2357#record, intake-2359#record, intake-2378#record.
- **M13 — redundant STT runner arms (durable trigger, no task).** Trigger: CS-8.STT4 leaves fewer than two runners passing gfx90a identity, or the two passing runners disagree on the Parakeet ultra versus base ordering beyond the interval in CS-8.STT5. Then, in that order: the s2s.cpp ASR-only HIP build (add hip to the CMake backend loop; est. 0.5 day) with parity against transformers; an ONNX Runtime CPU re-run of ultra versus base on FLEURS with both normalisers and symmetric unk handling; the sherpa-onnx int8 Parakeet/Nemotron STT arm; a MIGraphX probe only if the ggml HIP path fails entirely. Sources: intake-2350#record, intake-2351#record, intake-2379#record.
- **M14 — CPU degraded-path alternatives (durable trigger, no task).** Trigger: CS-26.F1 finds no ggml arm whose co-run p95 RTF stays below 0.5 in both windows. Then: the int8 ultra ONNX encoder on ONNX Runtime CPU (RTF and WER against the whisper CPU fallback); sherpa-onnx as one process hosting VAD, STT, LID and a TTS with pinned threads and an ORT intra-op spinning A/B (including the 5-minute idle-spin probe); CrispASR as a single-process coexistence arm. Each with A/B/A neighbour tok/s and per-thread CPU time (VB-SPEECH-CONV-1E). Sources: intake-2351#record, intake-2378#record, intake-2379#record.
- **M15 — English-only STT lane (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers: CS-8.STT6 recommends an English-only lane (the language gate splits by language), or ConwayResearch publishes scoring code, a leaderboard submission or the full-precision model. Then: Bark 3.0 Plus versus Clean on the CS-4 hesitation set, logging every deleted token outside a closed filler lexicon plus negation, number and entity deletions. The ternary redux model stays declined for production on French quality; it re-enters only if a public decoder reaches parity on French. Sources: intake-2341#record, intake-2342#record, intake-2343#record.
- **M16 — lexicon follow-ons (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers and their steps: (a) CS-8.LEX2 passes its decision rule: build TDT word boosting in a NeMo-Speech.cpp fork (remove the TDT throw in the joint tail, bias the token slice, pass logit_bias through the TDT argmax paths, share the tree helper, reset per utterance; est. 250-400 LOC), with token-identical parity against NeMo GPUBoostingTreeModel on its CPU path on 20 clips; (b) CS-8.LEX2 shows non-list-word WER degradation: add the unk-score knob to the trie (est. 30-60 LOC) and sweep it; (c) misses attributable to casing: case-insensitive matching (est. 150-300 LOC); (d) greedy recall short of target: a beam decoder with predictor-state tree (est. 600-1000 LOC) or a CTC-WS arm; (e) the Contextual Earnings-22 card or paper revision changes: revisit the clip-count difference; (f) the TDT build passes parity: the operator decides whether to file it upstream (outbound action); (g) CS-4 domain clips are recorded and boosting leaves residual term errors: a post-decode substitution pilot mined on 70 percent and held out on 30 percent, with the exact-value set unchanged. Sources: intake-2358#record, intake-2366#record, intake-2367#record, intake-2374#record, intake-2375#record.
- **M17 — TTS language-gate contingency (durable trigger, no task).** Trigger: the operator Italian listen check (CS-8.L) rejects Italian, or CS-8.G1B fires for de or fr. Then, for the failing language only: MagpieTTS Multilingual Q8_0 (CPU through audio.cpp or NeMo-Speech.cpp) and OmniVoice (omnivoice.cpp Q8_0 at 32 and 16 steps on the MI210, on the ggml verified by CS-43.1) synthesise the CS-4 language set, round-trip through the frozen whisper oracle under Annex S, plus a blind native-speaker check; the winner becomes the per-language fallback in CS-26 only for that language. If Magpie is chosen: its CPU first-audio and RTF at 1/2/4 streams under CS-26, and an audio.cpp gfx90a build (est. 1-2 days); audio.cpp as a one-engine host only if Voxtral or Magpie choose it. OmniVoice chunk latency on the MI210 is measured only once it is a candidate. Licences need an operator reading before shipped use. Sources: intake-2370#record, intake-2381#record, intake-2384#record, intake-2385#record.
- **M18 — STT adaptation and the CS-42 regression guard (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers: a CS-8.STT6 winner whose WER on a CS-4 domain subset is the binding gap; CS-42 opens; or NVIDIA publishes the promised fine-tune skill or repository. Then: every fine-tune run includes a replay slice of the other supported languages and modalities and is gated on a paired held-out non-target regression check at the deployment setting (threshold and replay ratio pre-registered; hard stop on regression beyond the noise floor; identical decoder, quant and normaliser before and after); an AdamSPD versus LoRA versus replay-only forgetting ablation on the CS-4 paralinguistic and routing sets; NeMo training on ROCm 6.2 is measured before any compute estimate is trusted (the dive estimate is unmeasured planning input). Sources: intake-2355#record, intake-2358#record, intake-2368#record.
- **M19 — VAD and turn-taking watch (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers and steps: FLEURS-VAD-102 is released (screen the gate arms per language on it/de/fr/en); CS-22.V1 shows music or instrument false alarms (FireRed's multi-label AED as a pre-filter); Silero and FireRed both fail CS-22.V1 (MarbleNet through CrispASR as a fourth arm); CS-22.T1 shows the text arm beats silence-only on false cut (the TEN Turn Detection weights as a reference point, after the operator reads the Agora licence conditions and a licence-register note is written); the guynich labels are questioned (audit the labelling code); upstream Silero ships its non-streaming model or resolves #798, or TEN resolves #79/#84/#85/#89. Sources: intake-2360#record, intake-2363#record, intake-2364#record, intake-2376#record, intake-2378#record, intake-2380#record.
- **M20 — runtime host and sampling (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers: CS-8.STT6 recommends a model that only transcribe.cpp serves (then a thin CS-20 wrapper over its C API: OpenAI-compatible /v1/audio/transcriptions, resident model, request queue; est. 1-2 days); qwentts sampling is tuned (then re-test voice quality, since defaults are stated bit-exact with the previous build). Sources: intake-2359#record, intake-2361#record.
- **M21 — benchmark provenance and set design (durable trigger, no task).** Triggers: OP-ESB-SPLIT is answered (relabel the Earnings22-Cleaned-AA anchor clean or train-overlap); a multilingual FD-Bench-style set, a licence change or a human-validated v2 appears (re-read for CS-36); CS-4 paralinguistic set design starts (score speaker identity with dedicated speaker-verification embeddings rather than an audio-language judge; verify the source section before citing). Sources: intake-2353#record, intake-2365#record, intake-2382#record.
- **M22 — STT serving leg on card 2 (durable trigger, no task).** Trigger: CS-7 is done (the second MI210 installed and verified) AND CS-8.STT6 recommends an STT arm (or the operator chooses one for CS-21). Then the arm joins CS-9's co-residency arms beside 35B-A3B: VRAM sampled during the run, sustained RTF and end-of-speech-to-final latency, against the CS-12 acceptance (STT RTF < 0.5) and the whisper cascade on the same card. Sources: intake-2344#record, intake-2347#record.
- **M23 — multi-speaker STT (durable trigger, no task; from the wave-3 steering).** Trigger: multi-speaker or diarised input enters scope (a CS-4 set with overlapping talkers, or the D7 remote-use design). Then re-intake nvidia multitalker-parakeet-streaming.

### Knowledge kept without work (K4 .. K7)

- **K4 — STT evidence rules and candidate facts (intake-underdog-bark-20261021; knowledge, no task).** Vendor cards, leaderboards, hosted boards, author timings and third-party ports are literature-class: they rank nothing and gate nothing; every arm is measured by our own harness on our own clips (intake-2349#record, intake-2350#record, intake-2355#record, intake-2366#record, intake-2367#record, intake-2368#record, intake-2371#record, intake-2373#record, intake-2374#record, intake-2382#record, intake-2383#record, intake-2385#record). Any "Open ASR leaderboard average" cited anywhere carries date, set list, reference track, scorer sha and the number of columns averaged (intake-2346#record, intake-2352#record); a cleaned-minus-standard delta is never quoted as a reference effect (intake-2353#record). Cleaned references are a different utterance set; the cleaned view of a turn is derived downstream from verbatim text and never fed into the voice turn (intake-2341#record, intake-2346#record). Long-form WER depends on the segmenter (intake-2343#record). Three NVIDIA transducer lineages exist (offline multilingual v3, cache-aware streaming en/3.5, offline+buffered unified-en), one checkpoint serves all chunk settings (intake-2356#record, intake-2369#record). The frozen whisper model is the least-fitted oracle among the tested models, English evidence only (intake-2372#record). Licence ledger for candidates: NVIDIA Open Model License (unified-en, Magpie), OpenMDW-1.1 (Nemotron 3.5), non-commercial weights (OmniVoice), Apache-2.0 plus Agora conditions (TEN), non-commercial data and code (FD-Bench); shipped use of any of them needs an operator reading, measurement does not (intake-2348#record, intake-2355#record, intake-2364#record, intake-2365#record, intake-2381#record, intake-2384#record). Custody precedents: withheld human-adjudicated sets are the field direction (intake-2373#record, intake-2382#record); operator-recorded clips are voice data, so privacy also requires keeping them out of public repos (intake-2354#record).
- **K5 — speech runtime and kernel facts (knowledge, no task).** Candidate runners add ggml generations 0.13.0 (parakeet.cpp), 0.25.1 (NeMo-Speech.cpp), 0.26.0 (transcribe.cpp, CrispASR fork) and 0.27.0 (s2s.cpp, upstream qwentts) beside v10 0.16.0, qwentts 0.17.0 and whisper 0.18.0: every launcher sets its own LD_LIBRARY_PATH and proves linkage (intake-2347#record, intake-2349#record). s2s.cpp serialises Parakeet behind one mutex and runs VAD on one CPU thread, so the controller must not assume batched STT (intake-2350#record). An adaptive encoder window for short whisper utterances exists in transcribe.cpp and is worth measuring only if whisper stays the cascade STT (intake-2359#record). v10 lacks llama.cpp #28957 but supports_op keeps argsort rows at or below 1024 on non-CUB builds, so production falls back to the CPU and needs no action; the upstream kernel was drafted by an AI agent and tested only on NVIDIA, which is why CS-43.1 is a real gfx90a test (intake-2362#record). audio.cpp is not prior art for that test (intake-2385#record). wavo is a cheap second implementation for diffing a suspicious transcript, not a measurement source (intake-2371#record). The CPU thread flag KVU-11c asks for already exists on `champion/speech-qwentts` `b1a1ec7` (`qwen-tts --threads`, `QT_CPU_THREADS`; SP2-FOLLOW-1 item 4); offering it upstream is an outbound action for the operator (intake-2361#record).
- **K6 — turn-taking and streaming priors (knowledge, no task).** Published WER-versus-lookahead curves for unified and cache-aware transducers are priors for the CS-22 chunk choice; CS-22.L1 measures ours (intake-2348#record, intake-2357#record). Silero v6 probabilities are lower than v5, so v5 thresholds do not carry over; re-check port parity when upstream moves past 6.2.3 (intake-2360#record). All FireRedVAD models are 0.6M parameters, so the gate runs on the CPU in both the normal and the degraded path (intake-2363#record). Per-stream cache memory for a cache-aware encoder is about 3 MB (dive estimate), so CPU concurrency is compute-bound (intake-2369#record). Consistency regularisation between a full-context and a restricted-context pass is a design note for the CS-28 port (intake-2357#record).
- **K7 — TTS interface facts (knowledge, no task).** The TTS server should hold one resident voice per language and take language per request, so the CS-20 TTS client addresses voices by (name, language) (intake-2370#record); an upstream-based qwentts exposes a per-request language field and rejects unknown languages, so the gate can be enforced at the endpoint once such a candidate passes (intake-2361#record). Magpie has five fixed voices and no cloning, so it cannot serve CS-27 persona voices (intake-2384#record). Full-codebook masking with an LLM-initialised bidirectional backbone is background only for any future custom TTS (intake-2381#record).

### CS-8.LEX2 dev lexical selection — 2026-11-01

The original hostfix2 dev grid reached `complete_raw`: 100 original clips × 15 cells, 1,500 native successes, zero native failures and zero exposed unknowns. The pinned dev-only scorer selected **NeMo local context, configured alpha 1** using the frozen rule (maximum pooled casefolded keyword F across all 12 NeMo cells, ties lower alpha then global). Selected folded P/R/F = 0.943396/0.558659/0.701754 (TP 100, FN 79, FP 6), versus alpha-zero F = 0.574803. Selected case-sensitive P/R/F = 0.942308/0.553672/0.697509. Term-masked casefolded lexical reference disagreement was 0.297696 selected versus 0.333026 alpha zero. Paired global-zero minus local-zero call-bootstrap 95% interval [0, 0], giving the predeclared lexical noise floor 0.0; it is not replaced by an invented floor. Selected-cell P-STT-1 v1 status was flagged on 35 of 100 rows; across the grid 561 cell/item flags are retained, and raw-token UNK is unobserved. Global alpha-5 pooled F is defined but its interval is unknown because one bootstrap replicate was undefined. These are LEX1 lexical reference-agreement results, not P-STT-1 v1 WER or a speech release claim. [belief: cc]

Full score `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/codex3-lex2-score-20261101/frozen-scorer/DEV-SELECTION.json`, SHA-256 `08c52ce9ba1d30dc3d3c6f0c56fbefb6ec1f13c8c1e31d8a041576502eb1c1a7`; compact all-cell summary [`DEV-SUMMARY.json`](../../artifacts/compute/codex3-lex2-dev-selection-20261101/DEV-SUMMARY.json), SHA-256 `a070c616910c2737ab4ce077a2bc3a199d335039a1378c9331c982df82f009a6`. The prospective source was published as root `386839550319bc4eebc5bb134f0f8c86a4bd6ff2` after synthetic capture, legacy adapter and synthetic planning-context controls. The historical raw grid predates its native capture hook: this conclusion is a committed observation, not a backfilled measurement tuple.

The actual selection generated a **not READY** heldout plan [`HELDOUT-PLAN.json`](../../artifacts/compute/codex3-lex2-dev-selection-20261101/HELDOUT-PLAN.json), SHA-256 `2fa8dc7764940c8486d959c7e36970a89f4f6f0d0d9b3e19f578e9c1f0cff16f`, binding original 772 test rows, 17 dev-call exclusions, 755 heldout rows, FLEURS, and the AA comparison. No test inference or score has occurred. A final selected/baseline native launcher, source capture, canonical engine same-entry normal and rc75 cutoff qualification and applicable disk admission remain required before heldout READY. Lexicon-free insertions per hour and the original heldout quality decision are unmeasured.


The offline scorer used a bounded guard: 5.44 seconds, sampled peak 77.6 MB, no inference. The full 11 MiB score file remains private at the exact retained host path and digest above. The compact summary preserves all 15 grid cells.

> ## ✅ TTS UNBLOCKED — 2026-07-31 — first working speech synthesis on this host, ever
>
> **The long-standing TTS blocker is resolved.** It was NOT resolved by the documented next step
> (diff C++ vs PyTorch codec tokens); it was resolved by replacing our port with the upstream one.
>
> **Root cause of the noise.** Our home-rolled Qwen3-TTS llama.cpp port hand-rolled the **codec
> half**. Upstream **`qwentts.cpp`** (ServeurpersoCom) implements the **full** stack — Talker, MTP
> CodePredictor, speaker encoder, and the complete SEANet / ConvNeXt / DAC codec. The unintelligible
> output came from the half we wrote ourselves. Cloned to `/mnt/raid0/llm/qwentts.cpp` (upstream tip
> `abab6b3`); builds **CPU** and now also **HIP for gfx90a**.
>
> **Measured — CPU, Qwen3-TTS-0.6B-base Q8_0 + qwen-tokenizer-12hz Q8_0** (1.19 GB total,
> `/mnt/raid0/llm/models/Qwen3-TTS-qwentts/`):
>
> | metric | value |
> |---|---|
> | **round-trip WER** (synthesize → transcribe with the incumbent whisper) | **0.0 % — word-perfect** |
> | **TTFA** (time to first audio) | **67.9 ms** — viable for streaming voice |
> | total wall for 5.84 s of audio | 6820.7 ms = **0.86× real-time** |
> | **`CodecDecode`** | **4362.9 ms = 64 % of total — THE bottleneck** |
> | Talker | 1313.7 ms |
> | CodePredictor | 1091.8 ms |
>
> The round-trip WER **is** the intelligibility check the archived handoff never got to run. The
> bottleneck is the **convnet vocoder, not the LM** — precisely the workload a GPU targets.
>
> **A GPU TTS bench is IN FLIGHT** (queued on the q3 GPU lane) → `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/tts_bench_results.txt`;
> harness `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/tts_bench.sh`. **No numbers exist yet — do not quote any.**
>
> **The one patch required is NOT a kernel change.** `qwentts.cpp/ggml/src/ggml-cuda/vendors/hip.h`
> guarded FP8 on `HIP_VERSION >= 60200000`, but the OCP-format `__hip_fp8_e4m3` type only exists from
> **ROCm 6.3**; we run **6.2.0-66**, which ships only `__hip_fp8_e4m3_fnuz` → `error: unknown type
> name '__hip_fp8_e4m3'`. Guard raised to `60300000`; free for us because **gfx90a has no FP8
> hardware**. This is a **third-party repo**. The frozen production kernel `production-consolidated-v8`
> @ `67a433bf4` is **untouched** and **no experimental-kernel handoff is needed** — CLAUDE.md's
> four-step workflow governs *our* kernel tree, which this patch is not in.
>
> **Do not revive the old C++ accelerator.** `llama-tts-qwen3` source is gone from the host; only a
> 2026-04-23 binary survives and it fails with `no backends are loaded`. Stock `llama-tts` misreads
> the weights as OuteTTS and emits unrelated tokens with no WAV.
>
> Historical blocker record: [`../archived/qwen3-tts-voice-synthesis.md`](../archived/qwen3-tts-voice-synthesis.md)
> (archived — carries a pointer to this resolution; do **not** resurrect it).
